seconds per streaming chunk\n\n\n# ── MI355X Hardware Constants ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\nMI355X = {\n    \"compute_pflops_bf16\": 1.3,\n    \"hbm3_bandwidth_tb_s\": 8.0,\n    \"cus\": 256,\n    \"wave_size\": 64,\n    \"lds_per_cu_kb\": 64,\n    \"vgpr_per_cu\": 512,\n    \"sgpr_per_cu\": 108,\n    \"mfma_block_sizes\": [16, 32],\n    \"xcds\": 8,\n    \"fp4_mfma\": \"mfma_f32_32x32x64_f8f6f4\",\n    \"scale_mfma\": True,\n    \"hbm3_burst_bytes\": 256,\n}\n\n\n# ── QiMeng-Style 5-Tuple Meta-Prompt Templates ────────────────────────\n\n@dataclass\nclass MetaPrompt:\n    \"\"\"MI355X-specific structured meta-prompt for kernel generation.\"\"\"\n    kernel_type: str  # \"gemm\", \"moe\", \"mla\"\n    tiling: str\n    reordering: str\n    vectorization: str\n    layout: str\n    pipeline: str\n\n    def render(self, strategy: str) -> str:\n        return f\"\"\"You are an expert AMD MI355X (gfx950) GPU kernel engineer.\n\nTARGET: {self.kernel_type.upper()} kernel optimization\nSTRATEGY: {strategy}\n\nHARDWARE CONSTRAINTS (MI355X / gfx950):\n- 256 CUs, wave64 (64 threads per wave)\n- MFMA: {MI355X['fp4_mfma']} with scale MFMA for MXFP4\n- 8 XCDs (cross-die clusters) — tile scheduling must be XCD-aware\n- HBM3 bandwidth: 8 TB/s, burst size: 256 bytes\n- LDS: 64 KB per CU, 512 VGPRs per CU\n- ROCm 7.1, PyTorch 2.10+rocm7.1\n\n5-TUPLE OPTIMIZATION DECOMPOSITION:\n\n1. TILING: {self.tiling}\n2. REORDERING: {self.reordering}\n3. VECTORIZATION: {self.vectorization}\n4. LAYOUT: {self.layout}\n5. PIPELINE: {self.pipeline}\n\nRULES:\n- Output ONLY valid Python code (submission.py format)\n- Must import from aiter, torch — no custom HIP compilation\n- Must pass correctness check (rtol=1e-2 vs reference)\n- Include type hints and minimal comments\n- The function signature must match: custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t\n\"\"\"\n\n\n# ── Per-Kernel Meta-Prompt Factories ──────────────────────────────────\n\ndef gemm_meta_prompt() -> MetaPrompt:\n    return MetaPrompt(\n        kernel_type=\"gemm\",\n        tiling=(\n            \"MFMA block: 32x32x64 for MXFP4. Tile M/N to fill 256 CUs. \"\n            \"For small M (4-64), use tall-skinny tiles (M=block, N=large). \"\n            \"BLOCK_K >= 128 for tl.dot_scaled. XCD-aware: total_tiles % 8 == 0.\"\n        ),\n        reordering=(\n            \"fp4x2 packing: 2 fp4 values per byte. E8M0 scale shuffle required. \"\n            \"B matrix pre-shuffled (bpreshuffle=True). \"\n            \"Consider fusing quantization into GEMM to eliminate 26µs quant dispatch.\"\n        ),\n        vectorization=(\n            \"Use gemm_a4w4 (ASM path) — 7-10µs for MXFP4 GEMM. \"\n            \"VGPR budget: 512 per CU. Coalesced HBM3 reads: 256-byte aligned. \"\n            \"Avoid 4D tensor ops (materialize penalty).\"\n        ),\n        layout=(\n            \"A: [M, K//2] fp4x2 (row-major). B: [N, K//2] fp4x2 (pre-shuffled). \"\n            \"A_scale: [M, K//32] fp8_e8m0. B_scale: [N, K//32] fp8_e8m0. \"\n            \"Output: [M, N] bf16.\"\n        ),\n        pipeline=(\n            \"Quant (26-84µs) → shuffle (1-3µs) → GEMM (7-10µs). \"\n            \"Bottleneck is quant dispatch. Fusing quant+shuffle saved 23.8%. \"\n            \"Explore: can we overlap quant with previous iteration's GEMM?\"\n        ),\n    )\n\n\ndef moe_meta_prompt() -> MetaPrompt:\n    return MetaPrompt(\n        kernel_type=\"moe\",\n        tiling=(\n            \"MoE has 2-stage CK pipeline: stage1 (gate_up GEMM) + stage2 (down GEMM). \"\n            \"block_m={32, 64, 128}. For 256-expert shapes with bs=16, most experts empty — \"\n            \"consider expert-skipping tiles. KSPLIT: 0 for CK path, 2-4 for cktile.\"\n        ),\n        reordering=(\n            \"moe_sorting_fwd reorders tokens by expert (coalesced access). \"\n            \"sorted_token_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids from sorting. \"\n            \"local_expert_mask parameter in sorting may skip empty experts.\"\n        ),\n        vectorization=(\n            \"CK ASM kernels handle vectorization internally. \"\n            \"For Python-level: minimize torch ops between stage1 and stage2. \"\n            \"SiLU activation applied in-kernel for split_k=0, else separate silu_and_mul.\"\n        ),\n        layout=(\n            \"hidden_states: [M, K] bf16. w1: [E, N*2, K//2] fp4x2 (gate+up fused). \"\n            \"w2: [E, K, N//2] fp4x2. Scales: [E, dim, K//32] fp8_e8m0. \"\n            \"All weights pre-shuffled (is_shuffled=True for MXFP4).\"\n        ),\n        pipeline=(\n            \"sort → quant_a1 → stage1(gate_up) → silu_and_mul → quant_a2 → stage2(down). \"\n            \"JIT builds: 128-260s (tight under 720s timeout). \"\n            \"sys.path fix needed for JIT build dirs on runner.\"\n        ),\n    )\n\n\ndef mla_meta_prompt() -> MetaPrompt:\n    return MetaPrompt(\n        kernel_type=\"mla\",\n        tiling=(\n            \"MLA decode: Q@K^T (bs, 1, nheads, 576) × KV (bs, kvseqlen, 1, 576). \"\n            \"Three-regime routing: matmul for small (bs<=4 OR total_kv<=32768), \"\n            \"aiter ASM for large. Tile by num_kv_splits: adaptive schedule \"\n            \"{1, 4, 8, 16, 32} based on total_kv.\"\n        ),\n        reordering=(\n            \"MLA fused KV buffer: K=576, V=512 (K≠V head dimension). \"\n            \"Q in [bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM=576]. KV in [bs, kvseqlen, 1, 576]. \"\n            \"V extracted as KV[..., :V_HEAD_DIM]. No transpose needed for 3D matmul.\"\n        ),\n        vectorization=(\n            \"Small shapes: torch.matmul (3D batched GEMM) — Python dispatch floor ~22µs. \"\n            \"Large shapes: mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd + mla_reduce_v1. \"\n            \"fast_mode=False is FASTER on MI355X (CU work distribution effect).\"\n        ),\n        layout=(\n            \"q_input: reshaped for ASM kernel. kv_4d: [bs, kvseqlen, 1, 576]. \"\n            \"Metadata: 8 tensors (work_meta_data, work_indptr, etc.) — cache these. \"\n            \"Intermediates: logits, attn_lse pre-allocated in cache.\"\n        ),\n        pipeline=(\n            \"Python dispatch floor: ~20-25µs per torch op. Leader at 33µs uses \"\n            \"single fused CK/ASM kernel with zero Python overhead. \"\n            \"Our 69.7µs = 3 torch ops + metadata. \"\n            \"Metadata caching gave 25% improvement (Phase 10).\"\n        ),\n    )\n\n\nMETA_PROMPTS = {\n    \"gemm\": gemm_meta_prompt,\n    \"moe\": moe_meta_prompt,\n    \"mla\": mla_meta_prompt,\n}\n\n\n# ── LLM Code Synthesis ────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ndef _call_ollama(prompt: str, max_tokens: int = 4096) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Call local Ollama with streaming to avoid timeout.\"\"\"\n    payload = json.dumps({\n        \"model\": OLLAMA_MODEL,\n        \"prompt\": prompt,\n        \"stream\": True,\n        \"options\": {\"num_predict\": max_tokens, \"temperature\": 0.7},\n    }).encode()\n\n    req = Request(OLLAMA_URL, data=payload, headers={\"Content-Type\": \"application/json\"})\n    chunks: list[str] = []\n\n    try:\n        with urlopen(req, timeout=OLLAMA_TIMEOUT_PER_CHUNK) as resp:\n            for line in resp:\n                if not line.strip():\n                    continue\n                data = json.loads(line)\n                token = data.get(\"response\", \"\")\n                chunks.append(token)\n                if data.get(\"done\", False):\n                    break\n    except (URLError, TimeoutError, OSError) as e:\n        logger.warning(\"Ollama unavailable: %s\", e)\n        return None\n\n    return \"\".join(chunks)\n\n\ndef synthesize_kernel(\n    kernel_type: str,\n    strategy: str,\n    context: str = \"\",\n    custom_meta_prompt: str | None = None,\n) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Generate kernel code using LLM with MI355X meta-prompts.\n\n    Args:\n        kernel_type: \"gemm\", \"moe\", or \"mla\"\n        strategy: High-level optimization strategy description\n        context: Additional context (previous attempts, error messages)\n        custom_meta_prompt: Override the default meta-prompt\n\n    Returns:\n        Generated Python code string, or None if LLM unavailable\n    \"\"\"\n    if custom_meta_prompt:\n        prompt = custom_meta_prompt\n    else:\n        factory = META_PROMPTS.get(kernel_type)\n        if not factory:\n            logger.error(\"Unknown kernel type: %s\", kernel_type)\n            return None\n        meta = factory()\n        prompt = meta.render(strategy)\n\n    if context:\n        prompt += f\"\\n\\nCONTEXT FROM PREVIOUS ATTEMPTS:\\n{context}\\n\"\n\n    prompt += \"\\n\\nGenerate the optimized kernel code now. Output ONLY Python code, no explanation.\"\n\n    start = time.monotonic()\n    code = _call_ollama(prompt)\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n\n    if code:\n        logger.info(\"Synthesized %d chars in %.1fs for %s/%s\", len(code), elapsed, kernel_type, strategy)\n        return _extract_python(code)\n\n    logger.warning(\"LLM synthesis failed, falling back to template for %s\", kernel_type)\n    return None\n\n\ndef _extract_python(text: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Extract Python code from LLM output (may be wrapped in ```python blocks).\"\"\"\n    if \"```python\" in text:\n        parts = text.split(\"```python\")\n        if len(parts) > 1:\n            code = parts[1].split(\"```\")[0]\n            return code.strip()\n    if \"```\" in text:\n        parts = text.split(\"```\")\n        if len(parts) > 1:\n            return parts[1].strip()\n    return text.strip()\n\n\ndef get_meta_prompt_for_kernel(kernel_type: str) -> MetaPrompt | None:\n    \"\"\"Get the MI355X meta-prompt for a kernel type.\"\"\"\n    factory = META_PROMPTS.get(kernel_type)\n    return factory() if factory else None\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Demo: print meta-prompts for all kernels\n    for kt in (\"gemm\", \"moe\", \"mla\"):\n        mp = get_meta_prompt_for_kernel(kt)\n        if mp:\n            print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n            print(f\" {kt.upper()} Meta-Prompt\")\n            print(f\"{'='*60}\")\n            print(mp.render(\"example_strategy\"))\n"
}

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**QiMeng 5-tuple decomposition**: The meta-prompts aren't just "try harder" — they constrain the LLM's search space. By explicitly decomposing optimization into tiling/reordering/vectorization/layout/pipeline, each dimension can be explored independently. This is why QiMeng reports 113x improvement over naive prompts — the LLM can reason about one concern at a time rather than juggling everything at once.

**Streaming for Ollama**: The code uses `stream: True` because on CPU-only systems (no CUDA/ROCm), non-streaming mode sends nothing until generation completes. With a 30b model at ~0.1-1 tok/s, that exceeds any reasonable timeout. Streaming accumulates tokens incrementally.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### Phase 2: K-Search Framework — DONE

**4 core files (951 LOC) in `luma_speedrun/autoresearch/`:**

| File | LOC | Key Features |
|------|-----|-------------|
| `ksearch_tree.py` | 300 | Adaptive K (MLA=12, MoE=8, GEMM=7), divergence (<80% best = prune), cross-kernel failure/success propagation, JSON persistence |
| `code_synthesizer.py` | 297 | MI355X meta-prompts (QiMeng 5-tuple per kernel), Ollama streaming, Python code extraction |
| `driver.py` | 217 | Cross-kernel learning loop, R-Zero plateau challenge, rate limiting, state persistence |
| `analyzer.py` | 137 | MI355X roofline model (1.3 PFLOPS / 8 TB/s), bottleneck classification, world model evolution |

### Phase 1 Task 1.4: Competition Rules — VERIFIED

- Deadline: **April 6, 2026** (not March 30 — that's registration)
- Scoring: MoE=1500, MLA=1250, GEMM=1000 pts. Score = MaxPoints × (1 - rank/20)
- All approaches are original work (required by rules)

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why this matters for competition strategy**: The non-linear scoring formula means rank improvements near the top are worth far more. Moving from rank 12→8 on MoE alone gains (1500 × 4/20) = 300 points. The research-driven approaches (HipKittens, GPU Kernel Scientist) target architectural breakthroughs that could shift rank by 5+ positions, whereas parameter tuning was yielding rank shifts of 0-1 positions. The skills now permanently encode this insight so future sessions don't waste time re-exploring dead ends.

     1→import math
     2→
     3→import aiter
     4→import torch
     5→import torch.nn.functional as F
     6→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes
     7→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     8→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
     9→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    10→from task import input_t, output_t
    11→from utils import make_match_reference
    12→
    13→
    14→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    15→# Constants
    16→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    17→MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE = 32
    18→PAD_ALIGN = 256
    19→
    20→
    21→def _pad_to(x: int, align: int) -> int:
    22→    return (x + align - 1) // align * align
    23→
    24→
    25→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    26→# generate_input: produce all tensors needed by ref_kernel
    27→#
    28→# Models DeepSeek-R1 MoE layer shapes:
    29→#   - d_hidden = 7168
    30→#   - d_expert = moe_intermediate_size (full=2048, or TP-split)
    31→#   - E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts (257 or 33)
    32→#   - top_k_total = nexpertspertoken + nsharedexperts (8+1=9)
    33→#
    34→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    35→def generate_input(
    36→    dhidden: int,
    37→    dexpert: int,
    38→    nroutedexperts: int,
    39→    nexpertspertoken: int,
    40→    nsharedexperts: int,
    41→    bs: int,
    42→    seed: int,
    43→) -> input_t:
    44→    d_hidden = dhidden
    45→    d_expert = dexpert
    46→    n_routed_experts = nroutedexperts
    47→    n_shared_experts = nsharedexperts
    48→    routed_top_k = nexpertspertoken
    49→    total_top_k = routed_top_k + n_shared_experts  # e.g. 8 + 1 = 9
    50→    E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts  # e.g. 256 + 1 = 257
    51→    M = bs  # number of tokens
    52→
    53→    # Padded dimensions (AITER MXFP4 requires 256-alignment)
    54→    d_hidden_pad = _pad_to(d_hidden, PAD_ALIGN)
    55→    d_expert_pad = _pad_to(d_expert, PAD_ALIGN)
    56→
    57→    config = {
    58→        "d_hidden": d_hidden,
    59→        "d_expert": d_expert,
    60→        "d_hidden_pad": d_hidden_pad,
    61→        "d_expert_pad": d_expert_pad,
    62→        "n_routed_experts": n_routed_experts,
    63→        "n_shared_experts": n_shared_experts,
    64→        "n_experts_per_token": routed_top_k,
    65→        "total_top_k": total_top_k,
    66→        "bs": M,
    67→    }
    68→
    69→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    70→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    71→
    72→    # ── hidden_states [M, d_hidden] ──
    73→    hidden_states = torch.randn(
    74→        (M, d_hidden),
    75→        device="cuda",
    76→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
    77→        generator=gen,
    78→    )
    79→
    80→    # ── Router: softmax top-k (routed experts only) ──
    81→    router_weight = torch.randn(
    82→        (n_routed_experts, d_hidden),
    83→        device="cuda",
    84→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
    85→        generator=gen,
    86→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
    87→    router_logits = F.linear(hidden_states, router_weight)  # [M, n_routed_experts]
    88→    scores = router_logits.softmax(dim=-1)
    89→    routed_weights, routed_ids = torch.topk(scores, k=routed_top_k, dim=-1, sorted=False)
    90→    routed_weights = routed_weights.to(torch.float32)
    91→    routed_ids = routed_ids.to(torch.int32)
    92→
    93→    # ── Append shared expert(s): always selected, weight = 1.0 ──
    94→    # Shared experts are indexed as n_routed_experts, n_routed_experts+1, ...
    95→    shared_ids = (
    96→        torch.arange(n_routed_experts, E_total, device="cuda", dtype=torch.int32)
    97→        .unsqueeze(0)
    98→        .expand(M, -1)
    99→    )  # [M, n_shared_experts]
   100→    shared_weights = torch.ones((M, n_shared_experts), device="cuda", dtype=torch.float32)
   101→
   102→    topk_ids = torch.cat([routed_ids, shared_ids], dim=-1)  # [M, total_top_k]
   103→    topk_weights = torch.cat([routed_weights, shared_weights], dim=-1)  # [M, total_top_k]
   104→
   105→    gate_up_bf16 = torch.randn(
   106→        (E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad),
   107→        device="cuda",
   108→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   109→        generator=gen,
   110→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
   111→    down_bf16 = torch.randn(
   112→        (E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad),
   113→        device="cuda",
   114→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   115→        generator=gen,
   116→    ) / math.sqrt(d_expert)
   117→
   118→    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
   119→    gate_up_weight, gate_up_weight_scale = torch_quant(gate_up_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   120→    down_weight, down_weight_scale = torch_quant(down_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   121→    gate_up_weight = gate_up_weight.view(E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad // 2)
   122→    down_weight = down_weight.view(E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad // 2)
   123→
   124→    gate_up_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(gate_up_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   125→    down_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(down_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   126→    gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(gate_up_weight_scale)
   127→    down_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(down_weight_scale)
   128→
   129→    return (
   130→        hidden_states,  # [M, d_hidden]                              bf16
   131→        gate_up_weight,  # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (raw)
   132→        down_weight,  # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (raw)
   133→        gate_up_weight_scale,  # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]         e8m0   (raw)
   134→        down_weight_scale,  # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]           e8m0   (raw)
   135→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,  # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   136→        down_weight_shuffled,  # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   137→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,  # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   138→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,  # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   139→        topk_weights,  # [M, total_top_k]                           float32
   140→        topk_ids,  # [M, total_top_k]                           int32
   141→        config,
   142→    )
   143→
   144→
   145→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   146→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   147→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   148→def _dequant_mxfp4(weight_fp4, scale_e8m0):
   149→    """
   150→    Dequantize MXFP4 weight to float32.
   151→
   152→    weight_fp4:  [N, K//2]  fp4x2  (raw, not shuffled)
   153→    scale_e8m0:  [padded_N, ceil(K/32)] e8m0   (M-dim padded to 256-align by dynamic_mxfp4_quant)
   154→
   155→    Returns: [N, K] float32
   156→    """
   157→    # fp4x2 -> float32 lookup: [N, K]
   158→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(weight_fp4)  # [N, K]
   159→    # e8m0 -> float32 power-of-2 scale: [padded_N, scale_K]
   160→    s_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # [padded_N, scale_K]
   161→    N, K = w_f32.shape
   162→    # Trim scale rows to match weight rows (scale M-dim is padded to 256)
   163→    s_f32 = s_f32[:N, :]
   164→    # Broadcast scale across block_size=32 columns
   165→    s_f32 = s_f32.repeat_interleave(MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE, dim=-1)[:, :K]  # [N, K]
   166→    return w_f32 * s_f32
   167→
   168→
   169→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   170→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   171→# will not run. only for reference
   172→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   173→def ref_kernel_pytorch(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   174→    """
   175→    Pure PyTorch reference: dequantize MXFP4 weights -> bf16 matmul -> SwiGLU -> matmul.
   176→    Uses the raw (un-shuffled) weights.
   177→    """
   178→    (
   179→        hidden_states,  # [M, d_hidden]          bf16
   180→        gate_up_weight,  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]  fp4x2
   181→        down_weight,  # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]    fp4x2
   182→        gate_up_weight_scale,  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]          e8m0
   183→        down_weight_scale,  # [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]            e8m0
   184→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   185→        down_weight_shuffled,
   186→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   187→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   188→        topk_weights,  # [M, top_k]  float32
   189→        topk_ids,  # [M, top_k]  int32
   190→        config,
   191→    ) = data
   192→
   193→    d_hidden = config["d_hidden"]
   194→    d_expert = config["d_expert"]
   195→    d_hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"]
   196→    d_expert_pad = config["d_expert_pad"]
   197→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
   198→    top_k = topk_ids.shape[1]
   199→    E = gate_up_weight.shape[0]
   200→
   201→    # Dequantize all expert weights to float32
   202→    # gate_up: [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad] -> trim to [E, 2*d_expert, d_hidden]
   203→    # down:    [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]    -> trim to [E, d_hidden, d_expert]
   204→    gate_up_dq = torch.stack(
   205→        [_dequant_mxfp4(gate_up_weight[e], gate_up_weight_scale[e]) for e in range(E)]
   206→    )  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad]
   207→    gate_up_dq = gate_up_dq[:, : 2 * d_expert, :d_hidden].to(torch.bfloat16)
   208→
   209→    down_dq = torch.stack(
   210→        [_dequant_mxfp4(down_weight[e], down_weight_scale[e]) for e in range(E)]
   211→    )  # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]
   212→    down_dq = down_dq[:, :d_hidden, :d_expert].to(torch.bfloat16)
   213→
   214→    # Split gate_up -> gate [E, d_expert, d_hidden], up [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   215→    gate_w, up_w = gate_up_dq.chunk(2, dim=1)  # each [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   216→
   217→    # Per-token MoE forward
   218→    output = torch.zeros((M, d_hidden), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=hidden_states.device)
   219→
   220→    for i in range(M):
   221→        x = hidden_states[i]  # [d_hidden]
   222→        for k in range(top_k):
   223→            eid = topk_ids[i, k].item()
   224→            w = topk_weights[i, k].item()
   225→
   226→            # Stage 1: gate_proj + up_proj + SwiGLU
   227→            gate_out = F.silu(x @ gate_w[eid].T)  # [d_expert]
   228→            up_out = x @ up_w[eid].T  # [d_expert]
   229→            intermediate = gate_out * up_out  # [d_expert]
   230→
   231→            # Stage 2: down_proj
   232→            # down_dq[eid] is [d_hidden, d_expert], .T is [d_expert, d_hidden]
   233→            expert_out = intermediate @ down_dq[eid].T  # [d_hidden]
   234→
   235→            output[i] += w * expert_out
   236→
   237→    return output
   238→
   239→
   240→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   241→# ref_kernel: calls AITER fused_moe with MXFP4 quantized weights
   242→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   243→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   244→    """
   245→    Reference implementation using AITER's fused_moe kernel with MXFP4 quantized weights.
   246→
   247→    Input data tuple (E = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts, total_top_k = routed + shared):
   248→        hidden_states:                [M, d_hidden]                           bf16
   249→        gate_up_weight:               [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   250→        down_weight:                  [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   251→        gate_up_weight_scale:         [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]            e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   252→        down_weight_scale:            [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]              e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   253→        gate_up_weight_shuffled:      [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   254→        down_weight_shuffled:         [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   255→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled:[padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   256→        down_weight_scale_shuffled:   [padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   257→        topk_weights:                 [M, total_top_k]                        float32
   258→        topk_ids:                     [M, total_top_k]                        int32
   259→        config:                       dict
   260→
   261→    Returns:
   262→        output: [M, d_hidden] bf16
   263→    """
   264→    (
   265→        hidden_states,
   266→        gate_up_weight,
   267→        down_weight,
   268→        gate_up_weight_scale,
   269→        down_weight_scale,
   270→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   271→        down_weight_shuffled,
   272→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   273→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   274→        topk_weights,
   275→        topk_ids,
   276→        config,
   277→    ) = data
   278→
   279→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
   280→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
   281→
   282→    output = fused_moe(
   283→        hidden_states,
   284→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   285→        down_weight_shuffled,
   286→        topk_weights,
   287→        topk_ids,
   288→        expert_mask=None,
   289→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
   290→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,  # MXFP4 uses per_1x32 block scaling
   291→        doweight_stage1=False,
   292→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   293→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   294→        a1_scale=None,
   295→        a2_scale=None,
   296→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
   297→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
   298→    )
   299→
   300→    return output
   301→
   302→
   303→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=2e-2, atol=2e-2)
   304→

     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→
     5→NOTE: Explicitly uses dynamic_mxfp4_quant from aiter.ops.triton.quant (patched in #975)
     6→      rather than going through aiter.get_triton_quant, which may dispatch to the
     7→      unpatched fp4_utils.py kernel. See ROCm/aiter#974, ROCm/aiter#975.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→import aiter
    11→import torch
    12→from aiter import dtypes
    13→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    14→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant  # #975-patched kernel
    15→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
    16→from task import input_t, output_t
    17→from utils import make_match_reference
    18→
    19→
    20→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    21→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    22→
    23→
    24→def _quant_mxfp4(x, shuffle=True):
    25→    x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(x)
    26→    if shuffle:
    27→        bs_e8m0 = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0)
    28→    return x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2), bs_e8m0.view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
    29→
    30→
    31→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):  # -> input_t:
    32→    """
    33→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    34→
    35→    Returns:
    36→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    37→    """
    38→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    39→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    40→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    41→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    42→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    43→    B_q, B_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(B, shuffle=True)
    44→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    45→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    46→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    47→
    48→
    49→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    50→    x: torch.Tensor,
    51→    w: torch.Tensor,
    52→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    53→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    54→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    55→) -> torch.Tensor:
    56→    """
    57→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    58→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    59→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    60→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    61→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    62→    """
    63→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    64→
    65→    m, _ = x.shape
    66→    n, _ = w.shape
    67→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    68→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    69→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    70→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    71→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    72→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    73→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    74→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    75→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    76→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    77→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    78→
    79→
    80→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    81→    """
    82→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    83→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    84→    """
    85→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    86→    A = A.contiguous()
    87→    B = B.contiguous()
    88→    m, k = A.shape
    89→    n, _ = B.shape
    90→
    91→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    92→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    93→    # A_q, A_scale = _quant_mxfp4(A, shuffle=False)
    94→    # B_q, B_scale = _quant_mxfp4(B, shuffle=False)
    95→
    96→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    97→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    98→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    99→
   100→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
   101→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
   102→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
   103→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
   104→
   105→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
   106→    A_q, A_scale_sh = _quant_mxfp4(A, shuffle=True)
   107→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
   108→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
   109→        A_q,
   110→        B_shuffle,
   111→        A_scale_sh,
   112→        B_scale_sh,
   113→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   114→        bpreshuffle=True,
   115→    )
   116→    return out_gemm
   117→
   118→
   119→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
   120→

     1→"""
     2→Reference implementation for MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter MLA kernels (mla_decode_fwd) as the reference.
     5→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA: absorbed q (576), compressed kv_buffer (576),
     6→output v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512.
     7→
     8→The input provides:
     9→  q:       (total_q, 16, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query
    10→  kv_data: dict with KV cache in three formats:
    11→    "bf16":  Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)  bfloat16          — highest precision
    12→    "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale — per-tensor quantized
    13→    "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0  — block-32 quantized
    14→  The reference quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly inside ref_kernel.
    15→
    16→The reference kernel quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly and uses fp8 KV (a8w8 kernel),
    17→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X with negligible accuracy loss.
    18→
    19→Decode only — persistent mode with get_mla_metadata_v1.
    20→"""
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    24→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    25→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    26→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import (
    27→    dynamic_mxfp4_quant,
    28→    e8m0_to_f32,
    29→    mxfp4_to_f32,
    30→)
    31→from task import input_t, output_t
    32→from utils import make_match_reference
    33→
    34→
    35→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→# DeepSeek R1 latent MQA constants (forward_absorb path)
    37→# https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1-0528/blob/main/config.json
    38→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    39→NUM_HEADS = 16
    40→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    41→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    42→QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64
    43→QK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM  # 576
    44→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK  # 512
    45→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM**0.5)
    46→
    47→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    48→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    49→
    50→# FP8 dtype (platform-specific via aiter)
    51→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    52→
    53→# Query dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    54→Q_DTYPE = "fp8"
    55→
    56→# KV cache dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    57→KV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    58→
    59→
    60→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    61→# FP8 quantization (sglang style: dynamic per-tensor)
    62→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    63→
    64→
    65→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    66→    """
    67→    Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization (following sglang scaled_fp8_quant).
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        tensor: bf16 tensor to quantize
    71→
    72→    Returns:
    73→        (fp8_tensor, scale) where scale is a scalar float32 tensor.
    74→        Dequantize: fp8_tensor.to(bf16) * scale
    75→    """
    76→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    78→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    79→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    80→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    81→
    82→
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→# MXFP4 quantization (aiter native: block-32, fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 dtypes)
    85→# Uses aiter.utility.fp4_utils.dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→
    89→def quantize_mxfp4(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    90→    """
    91→    MXFP4 block-wise quantization using aiter's dynamic_mxfp4_quant.
    92→
    93→    Block size = 32. Each block gets an E8M0 scale factor.
    94→    Two FP4 E2M1 values are packed per byte.
    95→
    96→    Args:
    97→        tensor: bf16 tensor of shape [B, M, N] (N must be divisible by 32)
    98→
    99→    Returns:
   100→        (fp4_data, scale_e8m0)
   101→        - fp4_data:   shape [B, M, N//2] in aiter_dtypes.fp4x2
   102→        - scale_e8m0: shape [B*M, ceil(N/32)] padded, in aiter_dtypes.fp8_e8m0
   103→    """
   104→    orig_shape = tensor.shape  # (B, M, N)
   105→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   106→
   107→    # dynamic_mxfp4_quant expects 2D: (B*M, N)
   108→    tensor_2d = tensor.reshape(B * M, N)
   109→    fp4_data_2d, scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(tensor_2d)
   110→
   111→    # Reshape fp4_data back to 3D: (B, M, N//2)
   112→    fp4_data = fp4_data_2d.view(B, M, N // 2)
   113→
   114→    return fp4_data, scale_e8m0
   115→
   116→
   117→def dequantize_mxfp4(
   118→    fp4_data: torch.Tensor,
   119→    scale_e8m0: torch.Tensor,
   120→    orig_shape: tuple,
   121→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
   122→) -> torch.Tensor:
   123→    """
   124→    Dequantize MXFP4 tensor using aiter utilities.
   125→
   126→    Note: dynamic_mxfp4_quant may pad both row and block dimensions in scale_e8m0.
   127→    We trim scales to match the actual data dimensions.
   128→
   129→    Args:
   130→        fp4_data:   packed FP4 data, shape [B, M, N//2] in fp4x2 or uint8
   131→        scale_e8m0: E8M0 block scale factors (possibly padded) in fp8_e8m0
   132→        orig_shape: original (B, M, N) for reshaping
   133→        dtype:      output dtype
   134→
   135→    Returns:
   136→        Dequantized tensor of shape orig_shape.
   137→    """
   138→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   139→    num_rows = B * M
   140→    block_size = 32
   141→    num_blocks = N // block_size  # actual blocks needed (e.g. 576/32 = 18)
   142→
   143→    # Unpack FP4 to float32: mxfp4_to_f32 expects (..., N//2) -> (..., N)
   144→    fp4_data_2d = fp4_data.reshape(num_rows, N // 2)
   145→    float_vals = mxfp4_to_f32(fp4_data_2d)  # (num_rows, N)
   146→
   147→    # Convert E8M0 scales to float32 and trim padded dimensions
   148→    scale_f32 = e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # (padded_rows, padded_blocks)
   149→    scale_f32 = scale_f32[:num_rows, :num_blocks]  # (num_rows, num_blocks)
   150→
   151→    # Apply block scales
   152→    float_vals_blocked = float_vals.view(num_rows, num_blocks, block_size)
   153→    scaled = float_vals_blocked * scale_f32.unsqueeze(-1)
   154→
   155→    return scaled.view(B, M, N).to(dtype)
   156→
   157→
   158→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   159→# Persistent mode metadata helpers
   160→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   161→
   162→
   163→def _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   164→    batch_size: int,
   165→    max_q_len: int,
   166→    nhead: int,
   167→    nhead_kv: int,
   168→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
   169→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
   170→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   171→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   172→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
   173→    num_kv_splits: int = NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   174→):
   175→    """Allocate and populate work buffers for persistent mla_decode_fwd."""
   176→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
   177→        batch_size,
   178→        max_q_len,
   179→        nhead,
   180→        q_dtype,
   181→        kv_dtype,
   182→        is_sparse=False,
   183→        fast_mode=False,
   184→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits,
   185→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   186→    )
   187→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
   188→    (
   189→        work_metadata,
   190→        work_indptr,
   191→        work_info_set,
   192→        reduce_indptr,
   193→        reduce_final_map,
   194→        reduce_partial_map,
   195→    ) = work
   196→
   197→    # Populate the metadata buffers
   198→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
   199→        qo_indptr,
   200→        kv_indptr,
   201→        kv_last_page_len,
   202→        nhead // nhead_kv,  # num_heads_per_head_k
   203→        nhead_kv,  # num_heads_k
   204→        True,  # is_causal
   205→        work_metadata,
   206→        work_info_set,
   207→        work_indptr,
   208→        reduce_indptr,
   209→        reduce_final_map,
   210→        reduce_partial_map,
   211→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   212→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   213→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   214→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   215→        fast_mode=False,
   216→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   217→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   218→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   219→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   220→    )
   221→
   222→    return {
   223→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
   224→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
   225→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
   226→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
   227→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
   228→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
   229→    }
   230→
   231→
   232→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   233→# Aiter reference kernel (decode only)
   234→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   235→
   236→
   237→def _aiter_mla_decode(
   238→    q: torch.Tensor,
   239→    kv_buffer: torch.Tensor,
   240→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   241→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   242→    config: dict,
   243→    q_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   244→    kv_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   245→) -> torch.Tensor:
   246→    """
   247→    MLA decode attention using aiter persistent-mode kernel.
   248→
   249→    Supports multiple Q/KV dtype combinations:
   250→      - Q_DTYPE="fp8":  fp8 Q + fp8 KV (a8w8) — fastest on MI355X
   251→      - Q_DTYPE="bf16": bf16 Q + bf16 KV (a16w16) — highest precision
   252→
   253→    q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576)  fp8 or bf16
   254→    kv_buffer:  (total_kv, 1, 576)         fp8 or bf16
   255→    q_scale:    scalar float32 (required for fp8 Q, None for bf16)
   256→    kv_scale:   scalar float32 (required for fp8 KV, None for bf16)
   257→    """
   258→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
   259→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   260→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
   261→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
   262→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
   263→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
   264→
   265→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   266→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   267→
   268→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D for aiter: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   269→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer.view(kv_buffer.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer.shape[-1])
   270→
   271→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   272→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   273→
   274→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   275→    meta = _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   276→        batch_size,
   277→        max_q_len,
   278→        nq,
   279→        nkv,
   280→        q.dtype,
   281→        kv_buffer.dtype,
   282→        qo_indptr,
   283→        kv_indptr,
   284→        kv_last_page_len,
   285→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   286→    )
   287→
   288→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   289→    mla_decode_fwd(
   290→        q.view(-1, nq, dq),
   291→        kv_buffer_4d,
   292→        o,
   293→        qo_indptr,
   294→        kv_indptr,
   295→        kv_indices,
   296→        kv_last_page_len,
   297→        max_q_len,
   298→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   299→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   300→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   301→        logit_cap=0.0,
   302→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   303→        q_scale=q_scale,
   304→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   305→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   306→        **meta,
   307→    )
   308→    return o
   309→
   310→
   311→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   312→# generate_input / ref_kernel / check_implementation
   313→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   314→
   315→
   316→def generate_input(batchsize: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int, seed: int) -> input_t:
   317→    """
   318→    Generate absorbed q and compressed kv_buffer for MLA decode.
   319→
   320→    Returns all three KV cache formats in kv_data dict:
   321→      kv_data = {
   322→        "bf16":  Tensor               — (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
   323→        "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
   324→        "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
   325→      }
   326→    """
   327→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
   328→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
   329→
   330→    total_q = batchsize * qseqlen
   331→    total_kv = batchsize * kvseqlen
   332→
   333→    # Absorbed query: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bf16
   334→    q = torch.randn(
   335→        (total_q, NUM_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   336→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   337→        device="cuda",
   338→        generator=gen,
   339→    )
   340→
   341→    # Compressed KV buffer: (total_kv, 1, 576) bf16 — the source of truth
   342→    kv_buffer_bf16 = torch.randn(
   343→        (total_kv, NUM_KV_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   344→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   345→        device="cuda",
   346→        generator=gen,
   347→    )
   348→
   349→    # Quantize KV to fp8
   350→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = quantize_fp8(kv_buffer_bf16)
   351→
   352→    # Quantize KV to mxfp4
   353→    kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = quantize_mxfp4(kv_buffer_bf16)
   354→
   355→    # All three KV formats: bf16 is a Tensor, fp8/mxfp4 are (Tensor, Tensor) tuples
   356→    kv_data = {
   357→        "bf16": kv_buffer_bf16,
   358→        "fp8": (kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8),
   359→        "mxfp4": (kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4),
   360→    }
   361→
   362→    qo_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * qseqlen
   363→    kv_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * kvseqlen
   364→
   365→    config = {
   366→        "batch_size": batchsize,
   367→        "num_heads": NUM_HEADS,
   368→        "num_kv_heads": NUM_KV_HEADS,
   369→        "qk_head_dim": QK_HEAD_DIM,
   370→        "kv_lora_rank": KV_LORA_RANK,
   371→        "qk_rope_head_dim": QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM,
   372→        "v_head_dim": V_HEAD_DIM,
   373→        "q_seq_len": qseqlen,
   374→        "kv_seq_len": kvseqlen,
   375→        "sm_scale": SM_SCALE,
   376→    }
   377→
   378→    return (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
   379→
   380→
   381→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   382→    """Reference MLA decode attention. Uses Q_DTYPE and KV_DTYPE to select kernel variant."""
   383→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   384→
   385→    # Resolve Q
   386→    if Q_DTYPE == "fp8":
   387→        q_input, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
   388→    else:
   389→        q_input, q_scale = q, None
   390→
   391→    # Resolve KV
   392→    if KV_DTYPE == "fp8":
   393→        kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   394→        kv_input = kv_buffer_fp8
   395→    else:
   396→        kv_input, kv_scale = kv_data["bf16"], None
   397→
   398→    return _aiter_mla_decode(
   399→        q_input,
   400→        kv_input,
   401→        qo_indptr,
   402→        kv_indptr,
   403→        config,
   404→        q_scale=q_scale,
   405→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   406→    )
   407→
   408→
   409→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-01, atol=1e-01)
   410→

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Reference implementation analysis — key observations:**

1. **GEMM ref**: Calls `_quant_mxfp4(A)` which does `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `e8m0_shuffle` as separate ops. The ref itself notes aiter has a Triton path (`gemm_afp4wfp4.py`) but we already know that's dead on the runner.

2. **MoE ref**: Uses `fused_moe()` with pre-shuffled weights — this is already the optimal API call. The `expert_mask=None` is interesting — the ref doesn't use expert masking. The `doweight_stage1=False` is correct (True crashes).

3. **MLA ref**: Uses `NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32` as a constant! Our adaptive splits schedule {1,4,8,16,32} based on total_kv already beats this. The ref also uses `fast_mode=False` — which we discovered is actually faster. But the ref uses `mla_decode_fwd` (the Python wrapper), not the direct ASM dispatch we're using.

**Critical finding in MLA ref**: The tolerance is `rtol=1e-01, atol=1e-01` — much looser than GEMM (1e-02) or MoE (2e-02). This means we have MORE room for approximate optimizations on MLA.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Contiguous A ensured once (reference calls it too, but we're explicit)\n\nCurrent: ~13.4µs | Leader: ~4.3µs | Gap: 3.1x\nBottleneck: quantization dispatch (~26-84µs) exceeds GEMM compute (~7-10µs)\n\nThe only path to beating the API ceiling is fusing quant into the GEMM kernel,\nwhich requires custom HIP compilation (blocked by runner source scanning).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport aiter\nfrom aiter import dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\nfrom aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n    # Quantize A to MXFP4 with shuffled E8M0 scales\n    A_q, A_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())\n    A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(A_scale_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)\n    A_q = A_q.view(dtypes.fp4x2)\n    # ASM GEMM — uses pre-compiled .co kernels on MI355X\n    return aiter.gemm_a4w4(\n        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,\n    )\n"

The 45µs gap requires CK/ASM bypass.\n\nKey rules (from 18 phases of experimentation):\n- NEVER use doweight_stage1=True (GPU fault or 82% mismatch)\n- NEVER use KSPLIT=4 for 32-expert shapes (catastrophic overflow)\n- fmoe_g1u1 is dead (NaN for 32-expert, no gain for 256-expert)\n- expert_mask crashes CK stage1 kernel\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport sys\n\n# JIT cache — may help with 720s timeout on repeated submissions\nos.environ.setdefault(\"AITER_JIT_DIR\", \"/tmp/aiter_jit_cache\")\n\nimport aiter\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n# Fix sys.path for JIT builds (runner installs to build/ subdirs)\n_AITER_JIT_BUILD = \"/home/runner/aiter/aiter/jit/build\"\nfor _mod in (\n    \"module_moe_sorting\",\n    \"module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_on_b16_silu_per_1x32_mulWeightStage2_\",\n):\n    _p = os.path.join(_AITER_JIT_BUILD, _mod)\n    if _p not in sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, _p)\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    return fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n"

Pre-allocated intermediates (logits, attn_lse)\n\nCurrent: ~69.7µs | Leader: ~33.0µs | Gap: 2.1x\nPython dispatch floor: ~20-25µs per torch op.\nClosing the gap requires a single fused CK/ASM kernel with zero Python overhead.\n\nMLA tolerance is loose: rtol=0.1, atol=0.1 — more room for approximation.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport torch\nfrom aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes\nfrom aiter import (\n    get_mla_metadata_info_v1,\n    get_mla_metadata_v1,\n    mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd,\n    mla_reduce_v1,\n)\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n# ── DeepSeek R1 MLA constants ──\nNUM_HEADS = 16\nNUM_KV_HEADS = 1\nKV_LORA_RANK = 512\nQK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64\nQK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM  # 576\nV_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK  # 512\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM**0.5)\nPAGE_SIZE = 1\nFP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8\n\n# ── Routing thresholds (Phase 11+14 tuned) ──\nMATMUL_MAX_BS = 4\nMATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768\nA16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144\n\n# ── Metadata + intermediate cache ──\n_cache: dict = {}\n\n\ndef _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:\n    if total_kv <= 2048:\n        return 1\n    if total_kv <= 16384:\n        return 4\n    if total_kv <= 131072:\n        return 8\n    if total_kv <= 524288:\n        return 16\n    return 32\n\n\ndef _quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    fp8 = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)\n    return fp8, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)\n\n\ndef _get_cached_metadata(\n    bs: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int,\n    q_dtype: torch.dtype, kv_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor, kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    num_kv_splits: int,\n):\n    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, q_dtype, kv_dtype, num_kv_splits)\n    if key in _cache:\n        return _cache[key]\n\n    nq, nkv = NUM_HEADS, work_info_set, reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map = work\n\n    get_mla_metadata_v1(\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        nq // nkv, nkv, True,\n max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen, uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,\n        fast_mode=False, max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,\n        intra_batch_mode=True, dtype_q=q_dtype, dtype_kv=kv_dtype,\n    )\n\n    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())\n    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # Pre-allocate intermediates\n    total_q = bs * qseqlen\n    logits = torch.empty((num_kv_splits, total_q, nq, V_HEAD_DIM), dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\n    attn_lse = torch.empty((num_kv_splits, total_q, nq), dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\n    output = torch.empty((total_q, nq, V_HEAD_DIM), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    meta = {\n        \"work_meta_data\": work_metadata,\n        \"work_indptr\": work_indptr,\n        \"work_info_set\": work_info_set,\n        \"reduce_indptr\": reduce_indptr,\n        \"reduce_final_map\": reduce_final_map,\n        \"reduce_partial_map\": reduce_partial_map,\n        \"kv_indices\": kv_indices,\n        \"kv_last_page_len\": kv_last_page_len,\n        \"logits\": logits,\n        \"attn_lse\": attn_lse,\n        \"output\": output,\n    }\n    _cache[key] = meta\n    return meta\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    qseqlen = config[\"q_seq_len\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen\n\n    # ── Regime 1: torch.matmul for small shapes ──\n    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS or total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:\n        kv_bf16 = kv_data[\"bf16\"]  # [total_kv, 1, 576]\n        # 3D batched matmul — no aiter overhead\n        q_3d = q.view(bs, NUM_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM)  # [bs, 16, 576]\n        kv_per_batch = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)  # [bs, kvseqlen, 576]\n        # Q @ K^T\n        scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_per_batch.transpose(1, 2))  # [bs, 16, kvseqlen]\n        scores.mul_(SM_SCALE)\n        weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1, dtype=torch.float32).to(torch.bfloat16)\n        # @ V (V = KV[:, :V_HEAD_DIM])\n        v_per_batch = kv_per_batch[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]  # [bs, kvseqlen, 512]\n mla_reduce_v1(\n        logits, attn_lse,\n        meta[\"reduce_indptr\"], meta[\"reduce_final_map\"], meta[\"reduce_partial_map\"],\n        qseqlen, output, None,\n    )\n\n    return output\n"

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MLA submission design choice**: The three-regime router uses OR logic (`bs <= 4 OR total_kv <= 32768`) rather than AND. This is non-obvious — AND logic would only use matmul when BOTH conditions hold, missing the case where bs=32 but kv=1k (total_kv=32k, which is better served by matmul than aiter's 50µs fixed overhead). The OR logic was discovered empirically in Phase 11 and provides a 6% improvement over AND.

**Metadata caching**: The reference allocates 8 tensors per `_make_mla_decode_metadata` call. Since these are deterministic for fixed (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen), caching them eliminates ~8 `torch.empty` calls per benchmark iteration. This gave 25% improvement in Phase 10. The cache also pre-allocates `logits`, `attn_lse`, and `output` tensors.

**MoE submission — the honest truth**: Our MoE submission is essentially identical to the reference with JIT path fixes. The API ceiling is confirmed — there's nothing more to squeeze out of `fused_moe` parameters. The 45µs gap requires architectural change.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now let me also look for potential new angles. The plan mentions AITER's `fmoe_fp8_block_scaled` — let me dig into what's available on the runner.

Here is the analysis.\n\n---\n\n## HipKittens Runner Viability: **NO**\n\n### How HipKittens Works\n\nHipKittens is a **C++ embedded DSL** compiled via **hipcc** (AMD's HIP compiler). The build pipeline is:\n\n1. **C++ source files** (e.g., `256_256_64_32_with16x32.cpp`) contain HIP kernel code with inline assembly and explicit register pinning\n2. **Makefile** compiles them with `hipcc --offload-arch=gfx9XX -shared -fPIC` using **pybind11** headers\n3. **Output**: a shared library named `tk_kernel<python-extension-suffix>.so` (e.g., `tk_kernel.cpython-312-x86_64-linux-gnu.so`)\n4. **Python usage**: `import tk_kernel` then `tk_kernel.dispatch_micro(A, Bt, C)`\n\n### Why It Cannot Run on the Competition Runner\n\n**Three blocking issues:**\n\n1. **Requires hipcc compilation at build time.** The Makefile invokes `/opt/rocm/bin/hipcc` with `-shared -fPIC` to produce the `.so`. The runner blocks `subprocess+amdclang++` patterns, and hipcc is a wrapper around amdclang++. Even if hipcc itself isn't literally string-matched, the compilation step requires invoking a subprocess to run the compiler, which the runner's static source scanning would catch.\n\n2. **Produces and loads `libamdhip64.so` dependent shared libraries.** The compiled `tk_kernel.so` links against the HIP runtime (`libamdhip64.so`), which is explicitly blocked by the runner's static scanner.\n\n3. **HK is NOT Triton-based.** The entire point of HipKittens is to bypass both Triton and hipcc's optimizer by using explicit register scheduling and inline assembly wrapped in C++ templates. There is no Triton JIT path. The paper explicitly criticizes Triton's limitations on AMD as a motivation for building HK.\n\n### Detailed Constraint Check\n\n| Runner Constraint | HK Triggers It? | Explanation |\n|---|---|---|\n| `hipModuleLaunchKernel` | Likely YES | pybind11 HIP extensions use HIP runtime launch APIs under the hood |\n| `hipLaunchKernelGGL` | Likely YES | Standard HIP kernel launch macro used by hipcc-compiled code |\n| `<<<>>>` | Possibly | HIP C++ syntax for kernel launch (hipcc translates these) |\n| `load_inline` | NO | HK uses Makefile pre-compilation, not `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline` |\n| `subprocess+amdclang++` | YES if compiling at runtime | hipcc wraps amdclang++; any JIT compilation attempt would trigger this |\n| `libamdhip64.so` | YES | The compiled `.so` dynamically links against `libamdhip64.so` |\n\n### What HK Is NOT\n\n- **Not a Python library** -- it's a C++ framework with pybind11 wrappers\n- **Not pip-installable** -- requires `git clone`, `source env.src`, `make`\n- **Not Triton-based** -- explicitly an alternative to Triton\n- **Not JIT-compiled** -- AOT compilation via hipcc + Makefile\n\n### Could It Be Adapted?\n\nNo practical path exists. HK's value proposition is direct hardware control via C++ templates wrapping AMD ISA instructions. The entire compilation and runtime stack (hipcc, pybind11 `.so`, HIP runtime) is blocked by the runner. You cannot translate HK kernels to Triton because HK exists specifically because Triton cannot express the optimizations HK provides (explicit AGPR/VGPR pinning, wave scheduling, chiplet-aware grid policies).\n\n### Sources\n\n- [HipKittens GitHub Repository](https://github.com/HazyResearch/HipKittens)\n- [HipKittens Dev-Open README](https://github.com/HazyResearch/HipKittens-Dev-Open/blob/main/README.md)\n- [HipKittens Paper (arXiv:2511.08083)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08083)\n- [Hazy Research Blog Post](https://hazyresearch.stanford.edu/blog/2025-11-09-hk)\n- [HipKittens Makefile (bf16fp32 GEMM)](https://github.com/HazyResearch/HipKittens/blob/main/kernels/gemm/bf16fp32/Makefile)"}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":319,"cache_creation_input_tokens":666,"cache_read_input_tokens":64469,"output_tokens":1133,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":666},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[],"speed":"standard"}},"requestId":"REDACTED","type":"assistant","uuid":"091189f0-d691-43fa-b00f-7f17023c9c78","timestamp":"2026-03-23T02:39:38.088Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion","sessionId":"35f70797-2df7-4280-bb95-2f01c9f8f473","version":"2.1.79","gitBranch":"session-repo-health-automation","slug":"expressive-greeting-sunrise"}

MI300X GPUs — ROCm Blogs\",\"url\":\"https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/aiter-mla/README.html\"},{\"title\":\"[Feature] [ROCm]: AITER Kernel Integration · Issue #14964 · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/14964\"}]\n\nHere are the search results for **ROCm AITER MLA kernels** (`mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd`, `mla_prefill_fwd`):\n\n## Overview\n\nAITER (AI Tensor Engine for ROCm) is AMD's centralized repository for high-performance AI operators. It includes specialized **MLA (Multi-Head Latent Attention)** kernels that dramatically accelerate inference for models like DeepSeek-V3/R1 on AMD Instinct GPUs.\n\n### Key MLA Kernels\n\n1. **`mla_decode_fwd`** (decode kernel):\n   - The AITER backends replace the decode kernel with hand-tuned assembly (`mla_decode_fwd`), which is where most of the performance gain comes from.\n   - AITER MLA for decode provides an impressive up to 17x performance boost, dramatically enhancing decoding efficiency.\n   - The 1.2–1.6x speedup primarily comes from the shared assembly decode kernel.\n\n2. **`mla_prefill_fwd`** (prefill kernel):\n   - The only difference between the two AITER backends is the prefill path: `ROCM_AITER_MLA` calls `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func` (AITER MHA automatically dispatches to CK or Assembly kernels), while `ROCM_AITER_TRITON_MLA` calls `aiter.ops.triton.mha.flash`.\n   - The prefill kernel difference between the two AITER backends has minimal impact on overall performance.\n\n### Integration into Frameworks (vLLM & SGLang)\n\n- vLLM now provides 7 attention backends on AMD ROCm software. The AITER MLA backends deliver 1.2–4.4x higher throughput (TPS) through AMD's AITER primitives and vLLM's kernel orchestration.\n- The integration of AITER into vLLM/SGLang for the DeepSeek v3/r1 model led to remarkable improvements—before AITER, throughput was 6484.76 tok/s; after, it increased to 13704.36 tok/s, marking more than a 2x improvement.\n- Key tracked PRs include AITER MLA support, AITER MLA on V1 Engine, AITER biased group topk for DeepSeekV3, and AITER CK 2 Stages MoE support.\n\n### Architecture & Design\n\n- DeepSeek and Kimi's MLA architecture compresses the KV cache to 576 dimensions (vs ~8K for standard MHA)—a 14x memory reduction. This compression changes the performance characteristics of attention, requiring a different optimization strategy than standard MHA.\n- AITER serves as a comprehensive hub for customer-level operator requests, with both C++ and Python APIs, enabling developers to focus on operator development while offering flexible backend kernel implementations using Triton, CK, or assembly. It supports inference, training kernels, GEMM, and communication kernels.\n\n### Benchmarks & Platform Support\n\n- Testing by AMD AI Framework team as of Jan. 29, 2026, measured inference performance in TPS on AMD Instinct MI300X, MI325X, and MI355X platforms.\n- All benchmarks were run using `rocm/vllm-dev:nightly_main_20260115` with ROCm 7.0.0.\n\n### Known Issues\n\n- A recent bug report (March 2026) shows Kimi 2.5 crashes by default on ROCm unless users set `SGLANG_ROCM_FUSED_DECODE_MLA=0`. This was tested on AMD Instinct MI355X with ROCm 7.2.0.\n\nFor hands-on usage, the MLA decoding attention kernel definition can be found in the AITER source code, requiring a minimum of eight input parameters with three additional optional inputs.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"a9599e9f-c38c-4040-a12a-b5c61a487543","timestamp":"2026-03-23T02:38:48.574Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"45c6092f-4a23-4b87-b02d-22c4603b60dc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion","sessionId":"35f70797-2df7-4280-bb95-2f01c9f8f473","version":"2.1.79","gitBranch":"session-repo-health-automation","slug":"expressive-greeting-sunrise"}

     5→{"parentUuid":"3324c79d-397f-4302-9839-598507c15513","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"6435371a-0a08-45e8-91cb-e903c4a5ba55","agentId":"aebf5f4cb61fce4a6","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"REDACTED","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"vLLM ROCm aiter fused_moe kernel wrapper integration new kernels 2026\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"[ROCm] Integrate AITER kernel for Fused MoE by SageMoore · Pull Request #14982 · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/14982\"},{\"title\":\"[FEAT][ROCm] Integrate Fused MoE Kernels from AITER by vllmellm · Pull Request #14967 · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/14967\"},{\"title\":\"Accelerated LLM Inference on AMD Instinct™ GPUs with vLLM 0.9.x and ROCm\",\"url\":\"https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/vllm-0.9.x-rocm/README.html\"},{\"title\":\"vLLM V1 performance optimization — ROCm Documentation\",\"url\":\"https://rocm.docs.amd.com/en/latest/how-to/rocm-for-ai/inference-optimization/vllm-optimization.html\"},{\"title\":\"Beyond Porting: How vLLM Orchestrates High-Performance Inference on AMD ROCm | vLLM Blog\",\"url\":\"https://blog.vllm.ai/2026/02/27/rocm-attention-backend.html\"},{\"title\":\"Releases · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases\"},{\"title\":\"Fused MoE Kernel Features - vLLM\",\"url\":\"https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/design/moe_kernel_features/\"},{\"title\":\"[FEAT] [ROCm]: AITER Fused MOE V1 Support by vllmellm · Pull Request #16752 · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/16752\"},{\"title\":\"[Feature] [ROCm]: AITER Kernel Integration · Issue #14964 · vllm-project/vllm\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/14964\"},{\"title\":\"ROCm Becomes a First-Class Platform in the vLLM Ecosystem — ROCm Blogs\",\"url\":\"https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/vllm-omni/README.html\"}]\n\nHere's a comprehensive summary of the search results on **vLLM ROCm AITER fused_moe kernel wrapper integration**:\n\n## Overview\n\nAITER (AMD's centralized repository) supports various high-performance AI operators for AI workloads. The integration of AITER's fused MoE kernels into vLLM for ROCm has been an ongoing, multi-PR effort tracked across several GitHub issues and pull requests.\n\n## Key Pull Requests\n\n### PR #14982 — Initial Fused MoE Integration\nThis PR is essentially the MoE portion of #14007 factored out. The main change was moving the AITER kernel selection logic out of the Triton kernels wrapper class and into the `UnquantizedFusedMoEMethod`. While not necessarily ideal, it was considered reasonable since the `UnquantizedFusedMoeMethod` is responsible for kernel selection logic. Ideally, a more sophisticated kernel dispatching system similar to `scaled_mm` would be added, but that was left as a follow-on task due to the significant refactor required.\n\n### PR #14967 — Broader Fused MoE Kernel Integration\nThe `ck_moe` kernel from AITER is integrated for unquantized model weights, enabled by default when `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER=1` is set, and can be controlled via `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_MOE`. This is suitable for MoE models such as Mixtral.\n\nThe `asm_moe` kernel from AITER is integrated for dynamic per-tensor quantization model weights, also enabled by default with `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER=1`, suitable for Mixtral FP8 quantization.\n\nThe `fmoe_fp8_block_scaled` kernel from AITER is integrated for block FP8 quantization, but is disabled by default even when the parent switch is enabled. Both the parent switch and its dedicated environment variable `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FP8_BLOCK_SCALED_MOE` must be enabled to use it.\n\n### PR #16752 — AITER Fused MOE V1 Support\nThis PR integrates AITER's fused Mixture-of-Experts ops to be used with V1. This PR is part of a larger effort to integrate AITER kernels into vLLM for improved performance on the ROCm platform.\n\n## Additional MoE Kernel Work (Tracked in Issue #14964)\nFurther PRs added AITER CK_MoE for Llama4, AITER 2-Stage CK MoE support, AITER MLA V1, and AITER MHA V1 integration. An upgrade to use AITER's fused MoE external API was also completed.\n\n## Configuration & Environment Variables\n\nAITER (AI Tensor Engine for ROCm) provides ROCm-specific fused kernels optimized for Instinct MI350 Series and MI300X GPUs in vLLM V1. `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER` is the master switch (defaults to False/0). Individual feature flags like `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_LINEAR`, `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_MOE`, etc. default to True but only activate when the master switch is enabled.\n\n## Recent vLLM Releases (2026)\n\nRecent AMD ROCm updates in vLLM include AITER fused RoPE+KVCache, MXFP4 MoE weight pre-shuffling on gfx950, CK backend for MoE quantization, and the AITER package being renamed to `amd-aiter`.\n\nIn the vLLM v0.12.0 and v0.13.0 releases, ROCm delivered major FP8, FP4, and low-bit improvements including native AITER FP8 kernels, fused LayerNorm/SiLU FP8 block quantization, MXFP4 w4a4 MoE inference, and optimized KV cache + assembly Paged Attention.\n\n## Performance & Benchmarks\n\nBenchmarks were run using `rocm/vllm-dev:nightly_main_20260115` with ROCm 7.0.0, a nightly Docker image built from the main branch of vLLM on January 15, 2026. Testing was performed by AMD AI Framework team as of January 29, 2026, measuring inference performance on AMD Instinct MI300X, MI325X, and MI355X platforms.\n\nThe AITER package introduced a new external API, `fused_moe`, which automatically selects and invokes the appropriate type of MoE kernel based on the specified quantization method. By integrating AITER's optimized MoE operators such as `moe-2stages` and `fp8-blockscale_g1u1`, the fused_moe API ensures efficient MoE execution.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"181cc1aa-5fde-4ece-9643-0e446f6f7363","timestamp":"2026-03-23T02:38:54.871Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3324c79d-397f-4302-9839-598507c15513","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion","sessionId":"35f70797-2df7-4280-bb95-2f01c9f8f473","version":"2.1.79","gitBranch":"session-repo-health-automation","slug":"expressive-greeting-sunrise"}

ROCm AITER MXFP4/FP4 MoE kernels:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n### ROCm 7.0 AITER MXFP4 MoE Kernels\n\nROCm 7.0 expands the list of pre-optimized kernels in the open-source AITER library, including the addition of MXFP4 MoE kernels. These kernels, custom built by AMD's engineers, can be easily dropped into users' code bases and are automatically enabled in the latest training and inference docker containers.\n\n### Hardware Support\n\nThe AITER library knows FP4 only works on gfx950 (MI325X/MI350X), but vLLM never queries this. This led to a notable issue where vLLM crashes on AMD MI300X/MI300A (gfx942) with default settings due to `VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FP4BMM=True`, since MI300X doesn't support FP4.\n\nQuark-quantized models can be deployed on AMD Instinct™ MI355 GPUs, which provide native support for FP4, FP6, and mixed-precision FP4-FP6 through Matrix Fused Multiply Add (MFMA) scale instructions.\n\n### vLLM Integration with AITER MoE\n\nThe ROCm AITER fused MoE backend implements AMD ROCm-optimized kernels via the AITER library, optimized for MI300 series, supporting FP8 W8A8 (per-tensor, per-token, 128x128 block) and MXFP4 W4A4.\n\n### MLPerf Performance Results\n\nAMD utilized Matrix Fused Multiply-Add (MFMA) instructions to implement MoE GEMMs for maximizing computational throughput, resulting in a 23% end-to-end throughput improvement. MXFP4 GEMMs account for about 62% of the end-to-end computation cost in the Llama2-70B model, and AMD profiled the workload to identify performance-critical GEMMs and optimized them with appropriate tile selection in AITER.\n\n### AITER Architecture Details\n\nThe AITER project covers FP4 and MXFP Support as part of its quantization operations, with each module compiled independently (e.g., `module_gemm_a8w8`, `module_moe_asm`, etc.). Kernel selection is based on architecture, shape, dtype, quantization, and performance tuning results.\n\n### AITER Release Activity\n\nRecent AITER releases include work on FP4 kernels such as \"Llama FP4 triton fusion + TP8 triton decode shape tuning\" and fixes to the \"fp4 gemm tune script\" along with adding \"ntile 128 for a8 blkQ moe 1 stage.\"\n\n### Composable Kernel (CK) MXFP4 Support\n\nThe `mfma_scale_*` instructions integrate dequantization with matrix multiplication, where each instruction takes separate scale factors (e8m0_bexp_t type) for A and B matrices, performing the computation: C += dequantize(A, scale_A) × dequantize(B, scale_B).\n\n### Known Issues\n\nUsers have reported issues running FP8/FP4 MoE with Expert Parallelism (EP) on MI355X with ROCm 7, encountering errors when trying to run `test_moe_ep.py --dtype=fp4x2`.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"ff303ece-7536-48fa-b921-d34b871f326d","timestamp":"2026-03-23T02:39:35.393Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c19aa15b-9799-4cbc-acb0-92983248db20","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion","sessionId":"35f70797-2df7-4280-bb95-2f01c9f8f473","version":"2.1.79","gitBranch":"session-repo-health-automation","slug":"expressive-greeting-sunrise"}

The AITER agent found important data. Let me extract the key findings from the MoE research.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**AITER has unexplored MoE kernels:**

1. **`fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1()`** — Block-scaled FP8 MoE. Disabled by default in vLLM. This is a DIFFERENT kernel path from our MXFP4 `fused_moe`. It uses FP8 block quantization instead of MXFP4 — the question is whether it's faster for our shapes and whether correctness holds against the MXFP4 reference.

2. **`fmoe_g1u1_tkw1()`** — Variant with "token-wise weight handling". This is NEW — not in our skill documentation. Could have different performance characteristics.

3. **`fmoe_g1u1_a16()`** — BF16 input variant. Might bypass the MXFP4 quantization entirely.

4. **`moe_stage1_g1u1()`** — First stage only of two-stage MoE. Could we call stage1 and stage2 separately with different parameters?

**Key constraint**: Our weights are pre-quantized to MXFP4 (fp4x2). Any FP8 path would need to either re-quantize or bypass the pre-shuffled weights. The correctness check is against the MXFP4 reference with rtol=2e-2.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Is it faster than fused_moe with MXFP4?\n\nStrategy: Use inspect to discover the signature, then call with our data.\nThe weights are MXFP4 but the kernel may need FP8 — we'd need to re-quantize.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport inspect\nimport sys\nimport os\n\nos.environ.setdefault(\"AITER_JIT_DIR\", \"/tmp/aiter_jit_cache\")\n\nimport aiter\nimport torch\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n# Discovery: find fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1\n_DISCOVERED = False\n_FP8_BLOCKSCALE = None\n\ntry:\n    from aiter.moe_op import fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1\n    _FP8_BLOCKSCALE = fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1\n    _DISCOVERED = True\n    print(f\"FOUND fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1: {inspect.signature(fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1)}\", file=sys.stderr)\nexcept ImportError:\n    pass\n\nif not _DISCOVERED:\n    try:\n        _FP8_BLOCKSCALE = getattr(aiter, \"fmoe_fp8_blockscale_g1u1\", None)\n        if _FP8_BLOCKSCALE:\n            _DISCOVERED = True\n            print(f\"FOUND via aiter: {inspect.signature(_FP8_BLOCKSCALE)}\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n\nif not _DISCOVERED:\n    # Also check fmoe_g1u1_tkw1 and fmoe_g1u1_a16 while we're at it\n    for name in (\"fmoe_g1u1_tkw1\", \"fmoe_g1u1_a16\", \"moe_stage1_g1u1\"):\n        try:\n            fn = getattr(aiter, name, None) or getattr(aiter.moe_op, name, None)\n            if fn:\n                print(f\"FOUND {name}: {inspect.signature(fn)}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"PROBE {name}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n\n# Fix sys.path for JIT builds\n_AITER_JIT_BUILD = \"/home/runner/aiter/aiter/jit/build\"\nfor _mod in (\n    \"module_moe_sorting\",\n    \"module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_on_b16_silu_per_1x32_mulWeightStage2_\",\n):\n    _p = os.path.join(_AITER_JIT_BUILD, _mod)\n    if _p not in sys.path:\n        sys.path.insert(0, _p)\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Fallback to fused_moe if probe kernel unavailable.\"\"\"\n    (\n        hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights, topk_ids, config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    # Always fall back to fused_moe — the probe is for DISCOVERY only\n    # On the runner, stderr will show the discovered signatures\n    return fused_moe(\n        hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights, topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n"
